([ThomasMoreAI/legal-skills-open](https://github.com/ThomasMoreAI/legal-skills-open/tree/HEAD/de/regulatory/skills/bgb-verhandlung-vergleich-und-eskalation) · ★136)
- **bgb-verhandlung-vergleich-und-eskalation**: Wenn es um BGB: Verhandlung, Vergleich und Eskalation in Fachanwalt Agrarrecht geht: entwickelt Verhandlungsziel, Vergleichskorridor und Eskalationspfad; liefert eine Verhandlungs- oder Eskalationslinie mit Optionen. ([vincentagwu/deutsches-recht-slim](https://github.com/vincentagwu/deutsches-recht-slim/tree/HEAD/fachanwalt-agrarrecht/skills/bgb-verhandlung-vergleich-und-eskalation))
- **bgc-accession-request-submission**: Use when a task requires submitting or simulating submission of a new biosynthetic gene cluster entry to MIBiG, or structuring BGC metadata into the accession-request → staff-approval → extended-annotation pipeline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-accession-request-submission))
- **bgc-biosynthetic-class-categorisation**: Use when a BGC metadata table (e.g. the 851 new MIBiG 2.0 entries) must be grouped by structure-based biosynthetic class to quantify composition, dominance of Polyketides/NRPs, or database growth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-biosynthetic-class-categorisation))
- **bgc-chemical-property-gap-quantification**: Use when auditing annotation completeness of a known-function BGC database — e.g. quantifying how many entries lack a chemical structure or have empty molecular formula/mass/chirality fields before and after a re-curation release. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-chemical-property-gap-quantification))
- **bgc-class-annotation-mapping**: Use when linking BGCs to MS/MS spectra requires a mapping between genome-side BGC classes and structure-side chemical classes, or when reproducing/ updating NPClassScore scoring tables from MIBiG entries. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-class-annotation-mapping))
- **bgc-cluster-export**: Use when you have completed a BiG-SLiCE v2 clustering analysis and need to convert the internal SQLite3 database results into human-readable, tabular TSV files for import into spreadsheet applications, statistical tools, or custom analysis pipelines that do not support SQLite3 directly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/bgc-cluster-export))
- **bgc-compound-crosslink-mapping**: Use when a curated set of BGC entries with compound name and structure annotations needs to be linked out to chemical databases (Natural Products Atlas, GNPS, PubChem) to build a linkage inventory per accession. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-compound-crosslink-mapping))
- **bgc-curation-channel-count-extraction**: Use when an article or report describes multiple curation channels contributing entries to a biosynthetic gene cluster repository (e.g. crowdsourced submissions, Annotathons, classroom curation) with numeric per-channel counts that must be tabulated and validated. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-curation-channel-count-extraction))
- **bgc-distance-metric-weight-optimization**: Use when BiG-SCAPE distance weights for JI, AI, and DSS must be calibrated per BGC class against known compound structures (MIBiG) so that BGC similarity networks reflect chemical similarity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-distance-metric-weight-optimization))
- **bgc-domain-architecture-similarity-comparison**: Use when input is antiSMASH-processed GenBank files of BGCs and the goal is pairwise domain-architecture distances, a similarity network, or benchmarking clustering quality (NMI/ARI/V-score) against MIBiG curated groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-domain-architecture-similarity-comparison))
- **bgc-euclidean-feature-vectorization**: Use when BGC datasets are too large for pairwise comparison tools (e.g., hundreds of thousands to millions of BGCs from antiSMASH runs) and GCF clustering must scale near-linearly, or when fragmented BGCs must be mapped onto prebuilt GCF centroid models via query mode. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-euclidean-feature-vectorization))
- **bgc-feature-extraction-from-antismash-output**: Use when a newly sequenced putative BGC — available as an antiSMASH output folder — must be placed into previously computed BiG-SLiCE gene cluster families (GCFs) to assess its function and/or novelty without rerunning a full clustering analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-feature-extraction-from-antismash-output))
- **bgc-feature-vector-normalization**: Use when when you have pre-computed BGC feature vectors (from domain architecture extraction) and need to cluster them into GCFs using BiG-SLiCE v2. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/bgc-feature-vector-normalization))
- **bgc-gcf-clustering-threshold-optimization**: Use when a BiG-SLiCE run's threshold parameter T must be selected or validated, e.g. when balancing the number of GCF models against fidelity to known MIBiG compound groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-gcf-clustering-threshold-optimization))
- **bgc-gcf-label-aggregation**: Use when paired genome–MS/MS samples need candidate GCF labels per MS/MS spectrum aggregated from kNN neighbor votes, and candidate links must be confidence-filtered against PoDP-validated gene–metabolite links. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-gcf-label-aggregation))
- **bgc-gcf-similarity-scoring**: Use when a new antiSMASH-annotated BGC needs to be assigned to existing GCF models from a prior BiG-SLiCE run (stored as an SQLite3 database), and a full re-clustering of the corpus would be wasteful. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-gcf-similarity-scoring))
- **bgc-gene-cluster-family-classification**: Use when you have paired genome-MS/MS samples (e.g. PoDP genomes plus LC-MS/MS spectra), a query MS/MS spectrum (GNPS reference or cryptic), and want to link it to a gene cluster family with precision ~0.929 top-10. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-gene-cluster-family-classification))
- **bgc-gene-cluster-family-clustering**: Use when you need biosynthetic gene cluster (BGC) detection and Gene Cluster Family-level absence/presence encoding across many bacterial genomes, as a comparative-genomics feature matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/bgc-gene-cluster-family-clustering))
- **bgc-gene-cluster-family-fingerprint-construction**: Use when paired genome-MS/MS samples have been antiSMASH-annotated and BiG-SCAPE-scored, and you need per-BGC genome-wide similarity vectors plus GCF labels as a KNN training matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-gene-cluster-family-fingerprint-construction))
- **bgc-homology-search-against-mibig-reference**: Use when you need to benchmark newly mined BGCs (e.g. from uncultivated soil bacteria or soil metagenomes) against a curated set of experimentally characterized BGCs of known function. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-homology-search-against-mibig-reference))
- **bgc-identification-from-genomic-sequence**: Use when you have assembled genome sequences (contigs or scaffolds in FASTA format) and want to identify putative BGCs and their precursor peptides before constructing a RiPP structure database for spectral matching. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/bgc-identification-from-genomic-sequence) · 3 copies)
- **bgc-metabolite-link-scoring**: Use when you have PoDP project metadata (JSON from Zenodo or pairedomicsdata.bioinformatics.nl) with validated BGC–metabolite links and need to compute and rank scores for candidate BGC–metabolite pairs in NPLinker. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-metabolite-link-scoring))
- **bgc-metadata-quality-audit**: Use when auditing a versioned repository of biosynthetic gene cluster annotations — typically MIBiG JSON entries — to quantify missing chemical structures, incomplete property fields, or annotation deltas between releases. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-metadata-quality-audit))
- **bgc-mf-link-scoring**: Use when you have preprocessed GCF-MF link pairs from paired genomics–metabolomics datasets (antiSMASH-detected BGCs clustered into GCFs, and GNPS spectra grouped into MFs) and need to rank them by likelihood of representing true natural product–biosynthetic gene associations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/bgc-mf-link-scoring) · 2 copies)
- **bgc-mf-link-scoring-standardisation**: Use when you have computed raw strain correlation scores and IOKR scores for the same set of GCF–MF (gene cluster family–molecular feature) pairs, and you want to compare or combine them fairly without one score dominating due to scale differences. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/bgc-mf-link-scoring-standardisation) · 2 copies)
- **bgc-molecule-linkage-analysis**: Use when identified molecules from GNPS microbial mass spectrometry datasets have paired strain genome data and you need to link molecules to biosynthetic gene clusters via MIBiG/BLAST validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-molecule-linkage-analysis))
- **bgc-msms-candidate-link-scoring**: Use when you have paired omics data (genome-derived GCFs plus GNPS molecular networking MS/MS spectra) and need to reduce long candidate BGC–spectrum link lists, e.g. via NPLinker with Metcalf scoring followed by NPClassScore class-matching. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-msms-candidate-link-scoring))

- **bgc-msms-link-filtering-threshold-application**: Use when a Metcalf-scored list of candidate BGC–MS/MS spectrum links must be reduced and prioritised using genome-based (BiG-SCAPE/antiSMASH) versus structure-based (CANOPUS/MolNetEnhancer) chemical class agreement. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-msms-linkage-analysis))
- **bgc-msms-linking-tool-benchmarking**: Use when comparing biosynthetic gene cluster-to-mass-spectra linking performance of multiple published tools against a gold-standard set of validated BGC–metabolite pairs from paired omics data. - **bgc-multi-locus-phylogeny-inference**: Use when antiSMASH-processed GenBank BGCs plus a chosen query gene are available and network-based GCF clustering (BiG-SCAPE) is suspected to have split or omitted related clusters — e. - **bgc-product-class-classification**: Use when you have MIBiG-labeled BGC training sets in Pfam TSV format (e.g. biosynthetic-class with 1355 samples, chem-act with 370 samples) and need to predict product class or chemical activity per BGC via multi-label classification with AUC-ROC evaluation. - **bgc-query-to-subcluster-motif-matching**: Use when tokenised (sub)Pfam-domain BGCs must be matched against pre-trained LDA sub-cluster motifs to predict biosynthetic sub-clusters, or to validate predicted sub-clusters against known SubClusterBlast entries. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-query-to-subcluster-motif-matching))
- **bgc-redundancy-network-filtering**: Use when a large tokenised BGC set (e.g. antiSMASH output with per-gene (sub)Pfam tokens) must be de-duplicated before motif/sub-cluster mining, and near-identical clusters (Adjacency Index &gt; 0.95 or full containment) should be collapsed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-redundancy-network-filtering))
- **bgc-region-post-processing**: Use when per-domain BGC detector scores (DeepBGC BiLSTM or ClusterFinder HMM) must be thresholded at a calibrated operating point (10% FPR or 80% TPR) and collapsed into discrete BGC regions for genome-scale precision evaluation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/bgc-region-post-processing))
- **bgc-repository-statistics-extraction**: Use when you need aggregate counts of curated BGCs — totals, per-class (e.g. Polyketide 825, NRP 627) or per-taxon (e.g. Streptomyces 568) breakdowns — from MIBiG or a similar curated BGC repository. - **bgc-sequence-domain-scanning**: Use when you have BGC sequences (in FASTA or GenBank format) from antiSMASH or other sources and need to extract biosynthetic domain composition as the primary signal for GCF (Gene Cluster Family) clustering or similarity queries. - **bgpdd-bugfix**: Fixes a localized bug on written evidence: a lint-gated bug report, Quinn's pre-fix RED capture, mechanical FAST/FULL/PLAN routing, Mason's and/or Nova's fix, Quinn's GREEN re-run of the same command, then a fresh Luna and the commit gate. Trigger phrases: 'fix this bug', 'debug this error', 'use bgpdd-bugfix'. ([MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills](https://github.com/MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills/tree/HEAD/skills/bgpdd-bugfix) · ★5)
- **bgpdd-bugfix-batch**: Fixes two to five independent localized bugs in one session by running the /bgpdd-bugfix contract once per bug in its own git worktree: parallel RED captures, per-route builders, a GREEN and a fresh Luna per bug, then each bug's own commit gate and one merge at a time. Choreography only — it restates no bugfix rule. Trigger phrases: 'fix these bugs', 'batch of bugs', 'use bgpdd-bugfix-batch'. ([MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills](https://github.com/MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills/tree/HEAD/skills/bgpdd-bugfix-batch) · ★5)
- **bgpdd-build**: Phase 2 of the Prompt-Driven Development SOP (Execution). Takes an existing implementation plan and executes it using Mason, Nova, Quinn, Luna, Dep, Cipher, Aria (advisory, blast-radius only), and Alex (re-planning loops only). ([MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills](https://github.com/MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills/tree/HEAD/skills/bgpdd-build) · ★5)
- **bgpdd-build-agy**: Phase 2 Execution exactly as /bgpdd-build, with one substitution: the builders (Mason, Nova, Max) run headless in the Antigravity CLI on Gemini 3.8 Flash High through the run_agy_builder.py gate instead of as delegated subagents, while Quinn (mid tier) and Luna (top tier) stay delegated. Choreography only — it restates no build rule. Opt-in; plain build requests stay on /bgpdd-build. Trigger phrases: '/bgpdd-build-agy', 'build with agy', 'build on gemini', 'use bgpdd-build-agy'. ([MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills](https://github.com/MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills/tree/HEAD/skills/bgpdd-build-agy) · ★5)

- **bgpdd-learn**: Captures lessons from the current session and routes them to the right layer — project rules file, agent persona, or methodology skill — via Forge's Learning Triage. Squad-internal: run by the main-session Orchestrator, never by delegated subagents. You write mini-requirements with the Orchestrator, Alex plans, Dep drafts the deployment manifest, then /bgpdd-build executes. - **bgpdd-quick**: The daily-driver lane for one small contained change — a rename, an added test, a small refactor, a one-file edit, a config tweak. Trigger phrases: 'quick change', 'small change', 'just rename this', 'use bgpdd-quick'. - **bgpdd-secure**: The standalone security assessment lane for an application that already exists. Derives a lint-gated attack matrix from security-and-hardening's Coverage & Provability table, the web OWASP Top 10, and the API Top 10, delegates Cipher (Ward for privacy and compliance rows, Quinn for browser-side probes) to execute safe read-only probes against the running application, and gates the results on runtime evidence — without running the plan/build pipeline. Audit-only, v1: no destructive payloads, no exfiltration, no denial-of-service, staging/local only. Security-only lane: product defects it finds… ([MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills](https://github.com/MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills/tree/HEAD/skills/bgpdd-secure) · ★5)
- **bgpdd-shipping**: Phase 3 of the Prompt-Driven Development SOP (Verification & Deployment). Orchestrates a dedicated Launch Squad (Vera, Cipher, and Dep) to execute the pre-launch checklist, harden the application, and orchestrate the final rollout. ([MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills](https://github.com/MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills/tree/HEAD/skills/bgpdd-shipping) · ★5)
- **bgpdd-verify**: The standalone verification lane for an already-discovered feature. Derives a lint-gated acceptance matrix from Echo's QA baseline (.docs/summary/{feature}/QA/manual-testing.md), delegates Quinn to automate it as permanent Playwright specs executed against the running application, and gates the results on runtime evidence — without running the plan/build pipeline. Verify-only: product defects it finds route to /bgpdd-bugfix. Use to regression-test any discovered feature repeatably. ([MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills](https://github.com/MuhammadShaharyarNaeem/blackgoat-agentskills/tree/HEAD/skills/bgpdd-verify) · ★5)

- **bgshells**: Use when you need to find, audit, or clean up Claude Code background shells (Bash run_in_background) — especially after a context compaction, when the task ids and pids of already-launched background runs are gone from memory. The harness can read/stop/wait on a background task only once you have its id, and exposes no way to enumerate what's still running, so leaked waiters (a stuck `until … do sleep; done` poller, an orphaned test worker) accumulate invisibly. This lists live background shells by asking the OS which processes still hold a `&lt;session&gt;/tasks/*.output` file open, correlates eac… ([mevanlc/my-ai-skills](https://github.com/mevanlc/my-ai-skills/tree/HEAD/claude-skills/bgshells))

([dreadnode/capabilities](https://github.com/dreadnode/capabilities/tree/HEAD/capabilities/bloodhound-enterprise/skills/bhe-bootstrap) · ★22 · 2 copies)
- **bhela-release**: Performs a full versioned release for the BHELA WordPress project. Triggers on requests like: "make release", "do a release", "release the project", "publish a new version", "create release". ([shauncuier/BhelaHouseBoatWP](https://github.com/shauncuier/BhelaHouseBoatWP/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/bhela-release))
- **bhela-test**: Run the BHELA regression suite (accounting, security, admin UI, OTP, SMS gateway) and report what broke. Use before any release, after touching roles, capabilities, pricing, cost sheets, the statement, salary or an admin screen, and whenever the user asks to run the tests. ([shauncuier/BhelaHouseBoatWP](https://github.com/shauncuier/BhelaHouseBoatWP/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/bhela-test))
- **bhengine-template**: Write, debug and ship a .bhe template for the bhex hex editor, in any directory. Use when writing a template that decodes a file format, editing or fixing an existing .bhe file, or debugging a template runtime error ("Exception @ line N"). Covers the bhengine language, its builtins, and the measure-then-read idiom that variable-length and text-oriented formats need. ([borzacchiello/bhex](https://github.com/borzacchiello/bhex/tree/HEAD/skills/bhengine-template) · ★8)
- **bhex-usage**: Drive the bhex hex editor from the shell — inspect, search, hash, diff, disassemble and patch a binary file, interactively or from a script. Use when answering "what is in this file", carving or extracting bytes, applying a binary patch, or automating any of that with `bhex -c` / `bhex -s`. Covers the command grammar, the current-offset model, backtick expressions and the write/commit workflow. ([borzacchiello/bhex](https://github.com/borzacchiello/bhex/tree/HEAD/skills/bhex-usage) · ★8)
- **bhived**: Search bhived's shared lessons before specialized, unfamiliar, risky, or medium/hard work, and write back verified fixes, dead ends, and version changes. Use it before you guess, after 2 failed attempts, when unsure about a version or API, on a confusing error, or when the user corrects you. Needs the bhived MCP server (bhived_query, bhived_write_instruction, bhived_write_mistake, bhived_write_update). ([ArtKeyAi/bhived-mcp](https://github.com/ArtKeyAi/bhived-mcp/tree/HEAD/plugins/bhived/skills/bhived) · 2 copies)
